The line of the fake agent comes first, because Ravel writes the JSON notice after the supervision attempt. The tables below give each member.

FieldValue and meaning
schemaVersion2, the version of the shape of the routing notice. The failure document has its own value, 2.
selectionAn object with provider, model, effort and reason. The owner supplies these strings. The labels do not connect Ravel to a model provider.
configThe configuration script: path, the canonical path of its entry, version, the owner’s VERSION string, and modules, the file measurements described below.
workerpath, the re-executed binary, version, its package version, buildId, its build identity, and rhaiVersion, its Rhai version. Ravel checks the worker identity before evaluation. This is not code signing.
selectionMsThe elapsed time of the evaluation of the configuration script, in milliseconds.
skillsnull with skill delivery disabled. Otherwise an object with descriptorPath, resolutionMs, defaultDirectory, sources, originalPrompt and prompt. defaultDirectory is the absolute default skill directory when it supplied the sources, otherwise null. Each source record holds id, type, resourceDigest and disposition, which is captured. originalPrompt and prompt each hold bytes and sha256. The member shows no prompt text, no skill text and no environment value.
contextnull when there is no delivered selection context. Otherwise, the report in the next table. It is never the session-result context.
boundsObjects named selection, context, source, sources, message, diagnostics and harnessRuns. Each has its field for the value with a unit (ms, bytes, sources or runs) and from. from gives default, fixed, settings.json or the flag that set the value.
configEnvironmentAn array of {name, set} grants. set is a boolean that shows whether the caller has the variable, never its value. Ravel does not add the base variables that ravel::env permits to this list.
resolutionA string that gives the resolved executable path and the base of the resolution: an absolute path, the caller’s working directory or a PATH entry.
promptSourceargument for --prompt. For --prompt-file, the file path joined to the caller’s working directory.
diagnosticsAn object with stdout and stderr, the captured streams of the configuration script as strings. Invalid UTF-8 bytes show as replacement characters.
operationReportsAn array of the report strings that the runner retained. It is present only when the runner returned its account of how the job ended. It is empty when there are no reports. A failed stop attempt in this list does not alone decide the final status.

The following arrays describe evaluation activity. They can be empty. Each record has all the fields in its row.

ArrayRecord fields and order
config.modulesname: canonical file path. bytes: source byte count. sha256: lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes. The entry comes first, then modules in first-import order.
readsvia: read_text or read_json. phase: load, context or selection. name: canonical path. bytes: count before the JSON parse. sha256: lowercase digest. Successful host reads appear in call order, also for repeated reads.
harnessRunsharness: settings entry name. status: the run status. exitCode: integer or null. signal: signal name or null. elapsedMs: integer duration. sentResult: whether the run sent a result. Calls appear in order, also for unavailable calls.

load means module top-level execution, context means load_context, and selection means select. The caller’s context file appears in context.sources, not in reads. Failed reads do not appear in reads, even when their bytes consumed a source from the budget.

A harness-run record contains no prompt, output, result text or token usage. Its status is exited, signalled, timed_out, output_limit or unavailable. An unavailable call has null exit code and signal, zero elapsed time and sentResult: false. See harness-run outcomes for the meaning of each status.

The non-null context report has six fields. They describe the data that selection receives. Chapter 4’s selection-context section teaches where they come from.

Selection-context report fieldMeaning
loaderWhether the load_context of the configuration script made the delivered document.
sourcesMeasured source records. Each has the canonical name, via (--context, read_text or read_json), bytes and sha256.
sourceBytesThe sum of the measured source byte counts.
encodedBytesThe byte length of the compact JSON encoding of the delivered document, with the measured records.
sha256The SHA-256 of that encoding.
valueThe delivered selection-context document.

Note: The notice can contain selection-context data and the captured output of the configuration script. Grant diagnostics do not show environment values, but the trusted script can still print a value itself.

JSON failure document

When a session fails with --json, the Ravel CLI writes a failure document on the caller’s standard error. Your program can read from it why the session failed and what to do next. A failed final write can leave a prefix of the result on standard output. Discard those bytes and use the failure status.

Fragment: the failure document of the third command of the example notices in chapter 5, “Read notices separately from results”. The configuration script has no rule for the kind deploy. This block omits the command and its status, 3. Here <scratch>/work stands for the example’s directory. In your shell, the document shows the absolute path of that directory, for example /Users/you/ravel-chapter-5/notices/config.rhai.

{"diagnostics":{"stderr":"","stdout":""},"error":{"code":"config_refused","configCode":"unknown_kind","exit":3,"input":"--kind deploy","message":"the configuration script <scratch>/work/config.rhai refused the selection: This configuration script has no rule for the kind deploy.","remedy":"Use the kind review.","source":"<scratch>/work/config.rhai","stage":"selection"},"schemaVersion":2}

A JSON failure document has schemaVersion: 2, an error object and an optional top-level diagnostics. That object contains the stdout and stderr strings of the configuration script. Ravel supplies the first part of the message. The configuration script supplies the configCode, the words of the message after its colon and the remedy.

Read code and stage together, and retain the complete standard error stream. Secondary operational reports can occur outside this document. See read failure notices.

error fieldPresence and meaning
codeRequired. A stable Ravel diagnostic code, such as malformed_input or session_result_missing.
stageRequired. A stage string from the table below.
messageRequired. An explanation. It can contain original and secondary operational details.
remedyRequired. A suggested correction or recovery action.
exitRequired. The numeric status. A cancellation also has its numeric status here.
inputOptional. The caller input involved, such as a flag.
sourceOptional. The file that relates to the failure, such as the entry of the configuration script.
locationOptional. The location inside the source or data, such as result.args[2] or a script position LINE:COLUMN. A host-request failure also names the function and field.
configCodeOptional. The refusal code that the owner supplied, beside Ravel’s config_refused.
boundOptional. The exhausted bound: name, the field for the value with its unit, and from.
signalOptional. SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP, the signal of the cancellation.

The stage tells where Ravel found a problem, not which process ultimately caused it. For example, Ravel uses evaluation for the whole deadline, because the Ravel CLI cannot see which operation of the script ran at expiry.

StageScopeTaught in
cliThe command line and the settings.Caller inputs, settings
authorityThe choice of the entry of the configuration script and the attempt to open it, or the query for the caller’s working directory.Define a configuration script
workerThe re-execution of Ravel as its Rhai worker, or the check of the worker and its protocol.Check and evaluate
evaluationThe evaluation by the worker as a whole, with the deadline and the cancellation of selection.Bounds, cancellation
loadThe import of the entry of the configuration script and of each module that it imports.Import other Rhai files
contextThe read, assembly, validation and measurement of the selection context.Supply selection context
validationThe check of the exports and the function parameter counts of the configuration script.Define a configuration script
selectionThe call to select and the check of the data that it returns.Select or refuse
resolutionThe search for the selected program.Program resolution
execThe preparation and supervision of the managed launch, and the decision about its outcome. This stage also covers failures of the result output or the result channel before the spawn.Results, notices
skillsThe capture, checks and storage of skill sources, and the prepared prompt of a launch. A settings problem keeps the stage cli.skills resolve

Environment variables

Variables that Ravel and the configuration script read

Which caller variables can the configuration script read, and how? The Rhai worker, the separate process that evaluates the script, receives an empty environment. The script calls ravel::env(name) to ask for a caller value. The worker sends that request to the Ravel CLI over a private socket. The Ravel CLI checks the name and answers from the caller’s environment.

The helper permits the base names below and explicit grants. A permitted but unset name returns (), Rhai’s empty value.

An ungranted name throws env_not_granted, even when unset. A value outside UTF-8 throws env_not_utf8. See environment grants.

Variable or classMeaning and effect
HOMELocates the default configuration script and settings. The default entry needs an absolute value. With --config, an absent or relative value supplies no settings. The helper permits it.
PATHResolves a bare program for a final selection or harness run. If unset, Ravel cannot search. An empty or relative entry resolves from the caller’s working directory. The helper permits it.
TMPDIRChooses the system temporary directory for the private worker directories and for result channels without --exit-dir. The helper permits it.
LANG, LC_*Caller locale values that the helper permits. LC_* means every name with that case-sensitive prefix.
Explicit grantsconfigEnv and --config-env add names that the helper permits. Duplicates count once. The caller supplies each value.
XDG_CONFIG_HOMEDoes not move the configuration script or the settings from $HOME/.config/ravel. It needs an explicit grant for ravel::env.
RAVEL_CONFIG_RUNMarks an evaluation harness run. Its presence, even with an empty value, makes start-session refuse before it reads the settings or evaluates the configuration script.

A grant controls the helper, not file access or the environment of a harness run. The trusted configuration script can read a credential file through a measured read, or receive credentials that a harness prints. Ravel does not redact that data.

Names that an environment grant cannot use

Some names control the dynamic loader or Ravel itself. configEnv and --config-env do not accept a name in these four prefix classes.

The match is case-sensitive. * means any suffix, also the empty suffix. It is not shell expansion. A grant that names one of them reports excluded_grant with status 2. Other names, such as NODE_OPTIONS, are names that you can grant.

The reserved classes are rules for ravel::env grants. They do not tell which names Ravel removes from the environment of the managed process. The next section gives those six exact names. See grant environment names.

ClassWhy Ravel reserves it
LD_*Dynamic-loader controls. They can make a program load other libraries.
DYLD_*macOS dynamic-loader controls, with the same risk.
RAVEL_*Ravel’s own controls. They include RAVEL_EXIT_FILE, the session-result context that the Rhai worker never receives.
HARNESS_DISPATCH_*Names of the earlier harness-dispatch command. Ravel reserves them with its own names.

Managed-process environment

Which variables does the managed process receive? When a process starts a child, the child gets a copy of the environment of its parent, unless the parent changes the copy. The Ravel CLI changes the copy for the managed process in three ways. It removes six exact names, and it adds a new RAVEL_EXIT_FILE. It also sets or removes RAVEL_SKILL_DIRECTORIES.

The managed process inherits the caller’s environment, except for six exact names that Ravel removes. The list of these names is below. Ravel then supplies its new RAVEL_EXIT_FILE. It also replaces or removes RAVEL_SKILL_DIRECTORIES, as the table below shows.

Other names stay inherited, for example any application credentials and the names in the reserved classes above. An environment grant has no effect on that inheritance. See caller environment.

Name or classManaged processTaught in
RAVEL_EXIT_FILEA new path for this session. Ravel supplies it after the environment grants.Nested sessions
RAVEL_RUN_ID, RAVEL_STATE_DIR and the three HARNESS_DISPATCH_* controls belowAbsent. They are controls of an enclosing session or of another launcher.Caller environment
RAVEL_SKILL_DIRECTORIESThe descriptor path of this session when skill delivery is enabled. Absent when it is disabled.Skill delivery at launch
Every other caller variableInherited. --config-env grants only ravel::env access and adds no variable here.What the managed process receives

The removed names are exactly RAVEL_EXIT_FILE, RAVEL_RUN_ID, RAVEL_STATE_DIR, HARNESS_DISPATCH_EXIT_FILE, HARNESS_DISPATCH_RUN_ID and HARNESS_DISPATCH_STATE_DIR. Ravel replaces the first with the new result-channel path. The other five stay absent. Each evaluation harness run uses the same rules, with its own result path and the additional RAVEL_CONFIG_RUN marker.

With skill delivery enabled, Ravel sets RAVEL_SKILL_DIRECTORIES to the absolute descriptor path in the managed process and in each harness run. That value replaces an inherited value. With delivery disabled, Ravel removes the name from both. The Rhai worker never receives it. A nested start-session sets the value of its own session only for its own managed process.

The name is in the reserved class RAVEL_*, so a grant of it refuses as excluded_grant, and ravel::env of it throws env_not_granted. A script reads request.skills instead. The variable grants no read access and no authority to end a session.

A final managed process receives no marker, because an invocation that inherits it refuses before selection. These obsolete identity and storage names create no store and select no session. See caller environment.

RAVEL_EXIT_FILE

RAVEL_EXIT_FILE is the inherited session-result context: the path that end-session uses to send the result to its enclosing session. The path names one result file in the private result channel of the session. The Ravel CLI sets it separately for each managed launch and evaluation harness run. The Rhai worker never receives it.

How does the path reach end-session? Ravel puts it in the environment of the program that it launches. Each child process gets a copy of the environment of its parent, unless the parent changes it. If each parent leaves the path unchanged, a shell that the harness starts and the ravel end-session in that shell have the same path. end-session reads the path from its own environment and writes the result to that file.

What happens when sessions nest? A nested launch replaces the path only for its own managed process. The value of the outer caller does not change. In the real run below, a fake agent printed its own RAVEL_EXIT_FILE. Then it was the caller of a second, inner session. The inner fake agent printed its value, and the outer fake agent printed its value again.

Fragment: standard error of the real nested session of chapter 1, on macOS with TMPDIR=/tmp. This block omits the cleanup reports. The random parts of each path change in each run. macOS gives /tmp the real path /private/tmp.

ravel: starting provider local, model fake, effort none for kind "outer"
outer-agent: RAVEL_EXIT_FILE=/private/tmp/ravel-session-mlcN86/signal-c7d5f2eed4198eb768996f7b4868bbcc
ravel: starting provider local, model fake, effort none for kind "inner"
inner-agent: RAVEL_EXIT_FILE=/private/tmp/ravel-session-QpztGn/signal-b643cbee529e5887c7d2a3d0496210c9
outer-agent: the inner session returned inner-done
outer-agent: RAVEL_EXIT_FILE=/private/tmp/ravel-session-mlcN86/signal-c7d5f2eed4198eb768996f7b4868bbcc

Each child inherits its parent’s path unless its parent changes it. Ravel sets a new path for the inner harness.

Figure: The nested run gives each session’s children its own result path.

Open the inherited result paths at full size.

The inner session got a new path, but only the inner fake agent received it. The outer fake agent kept its own path. Thus, each end-session goes to the session that encloses it.

If the path is unset, empty or stale, end-session fails with status 1. Do not use it as a persistent result location or as an address across sessions. The result channel separates ordinary sessions. It is not a defence against hostile code that runs as the same user. See nested sessions and the row for code of the same user in Know when Ravel cannot guarantee cleanup.

Configuration script and settings

config.rhai

Where does Ravel find the configuration script? The configuration script is a file of Rhai code that chooses the program for each session. The book calls the file that Ravel loads first the entry. The entry can import other Rhai files.

$HOME/.config/ravel/config.rhai is the default entry. This default needs an absolute HOME. --config PATH chooses a different entry, relative to the caller’s working directory. There is no repository search, environment override or fallback script. An explicit entry can have any file name that ends in .rhai, such as review.rhai.

The entry must canonicalise to a readable regular UTF-8 file of at most 1 MiB. Its canonical path must also be UTF-8. A symlink selects its real file. Imports resolve from the directory of that real file. An unacceptable entry gives status 3 before the script runs. See define a configuration script.

Entry choiceRavel 2.0.0 behaviour
Default Rhai entry existsEvaluate it. Other formats of configuration script do not participate.
Default Rhai entry absent, but policy.ts existsRefuse with config_format_retired, status 3. The remedy names config.rhai and chapter 4, “Write a configuration script”.
Neither default file existsRefuse with config_missing, status 3.
Explicit .rhai entryEvaluate it after file checks.
Explicit .ts, .mts, .cts, .js, .mjs or .cjs entryRefuse with config_format_retired, status 3.
Other explicit extensionRefuse with config_unreadable, status 3. Supply a .rhai file.

Ravel evaluates the entry in the Rhai worker, a second copy of its own executable. At the deadline, the Ravel CLI ends evaluation and can signal the worker. A kernel stall can delay its exit. A memory or stack failure of the script stops only the worker. The configuration script has no direct filesystem, network, process or environment access. It uses the host capabilities for permitted effects.

The worker receives no session-result context. Before the final managed launch, every host call ends and Ravel reaps the worker. Ravel also removes the worker’s private directory.

settings.json

You may want the same limits, grants and harnesses for every session of your account. The owner writes them once in a settings file. A command-line option can then replace some values for one session.

$HOME/.config/ravel/settings.json supplies the owner’s defaults for start-session. --config does not move it, and XDG_CONFIG_HOME does not select it.

Fragment: a settings file with four of the top-level keys, which the checkout build accepted on 2026-10-29. This block omits the configuration script and the program fake-classifier.

{"timeoutMs":60000,"configEnv":["BOOK_EFFORT"],"harnesses":{"classifier":{"program":"fake-classifier","capabilities":["classify"]}},"budgets":{"harnessRuns":1}}

These settings give evaluation 60 seconds and let the configuration script read BOOK_EFFORT. They name one harness, classifier, and permit one harness run for each session. contextBytes is absent, so its default applies.

A missing file, an absent or relative HOME or an empty JSON object supplies no settings. A file that exists must be a readable regular file that contains a JSON object. end-session reads neither the settings nor the configuration script. See set bounds and grants.

KeyAccepted value, default and consequenceTaught in
timeoutMsOptional whole number of milliseconds, 1,000–600,000 inclusive. The default is 30,000. --timeout-ms replaces the value after settings validation. It limits worker startup, module loads, the loader, selection and every host call. It does not limit the final managed process.Set bounds
contextBytesOptional whole number of bytes, 1–8,388,608 inclusive. The default is 262,144. --context-bytes replaces it after validation. It limits the caller document and the delivered selection context. It is also the ceiling for a host read.Set bounds
configEnvOptional array of environment-variable names. The default is no extra grants. Each name is nonempty UTF-8 without = and cannot match a reserved grant class. Flags add names. Ravel merges duplicates, and unset values stay absent. Grants permit ravel::env reads. They change no managed-process or harness-run environment.Set bounds and environment grants
harnessesOptional object of named programs for harness runs. Default: no entries. Each entry has the fields below. Final selections need no entry.Harness entries
budgetsOptional object with only harnessRuns. An empty object uses the default budget.Evaluation budgets
skillsOptional object with exactly schemaVersion and the optional sources, mandatory and resolutionTimeoutMs. Without it, or without sources, the default skill directory supplies the sources. The fields are below.skills resolve

A harness name contains 1–64 lowercase ASCII letters, digits or dashes. The first character is a letter or digit. Ravel refuses unknown nested fields.

Nested settingAccepted value and default
harnesses.NAME.programRequired nonempty string without NUL. An absolute path, a relative path with /, or a bare PATH name. Resolution uses the caller’s directory and environment.
harnesses.NAME.capabilitiesOptional array of nonempty strings. Default: empty. Discovery repeats these owner labels without interpretation.
budgets.harnessRunsOptional integer from 0 to 16 inclusive. Default: 0. Only settings can raise it. Each launched harness run consumes one.
skills.schemaVersionRequired. The integer 1.
skills.sourcesOptional array of source records, in the order of presentation. With it, the array names exactly the sources, and Ravel reads no default skill directory. [] with no mandatory file disables delivery.
skills.sources[N].idRequired unique token of 1 to 64 lowercase ASCII letters and digits. Single hyphens separate groups, and the first character is a letter.
skills.sources[N].typeRequired. local, the only source type. Ravel reads it before the other fields of the record. A missing value or another value refuses at this location.
skills.sources[N].pathRequired. An absolute UTF-8 directory path. Ravel expands no tilde and no environment variable.
skills.sources[N].rootsRequired nonempty array of search directories, relative to the source. . is the source itself. A repeated root refuses.
skills.mandatoryOptional array of records with exactly source and path, in the order to read them. source names the ID of a source in sources, or without sources a source of the default skill directory. path is a file, relative to that source, and . is not a file. A repeated reference refuses.
skills.resolutionTimeoutMsOptional integer from 1,000 to 3,600,000. Default: 120,000. It bounds one complete resolution, and a breach refuses as skill_resolution_timeout, status 124. No flag changes it.

A relative path in skills is normalised. It has no absolute start, no empty segment, no . or .. segment, no backslash, no control character and no separator at the end.

These problems report settings_invalid, status 2:

  • an unknown key, also the obsolete stateDir and a config key
  • an unreadable or non-regular file
  • invalid JSON, or a document that is not an object
  • a configEnv that is not an array of strings
  • a malformed harnesses entry or budgets object, also an invalid harnessRuns value
  • a malformed skills object or an unknown field in it
  • with sources, a mandatory record that names a source outside sources
  • a source type other than local, such as git. The remedy tells you to make any checkout of a repository with a tool outside Ravel, and to name its directory in a local record
  • a repeated member name in any object of the file, for every key

Invalid timeoutMs or contextBytes values and malformed grant names use malformed_input, and excluded grants use excluded_grant. Both have status 2. Ravel validates the whole file before flags replace values. Thus, an invalid setting fails even with a valid flag. No setting opens persistent storage. See settings validation.

Bounds

A bound is a limit on evaluation: on its time, on the data that it reads or on the harness runs that it makes. When a bound stops evaluation, Ravel launches no final managed process. Harness runs can occur before that stop. You can change some bounds in the settings or on the command line. Ravel sets the others, and you cannot change them.

The configuration script can read the effective bounds in request.limits. For example, with no settings and no bound options, a real request had this member:

Fragment: the limits member of the request in the request entry. This block omits the other members.

{"limits":{"contextBytes":262144,"diagnosticsBytes":262144,"harnessRuns":0,"messageBytes":1048576,"selectionMs":30000,"sourceBytes":65536,"sources":256}}

request.limits gives seven effective bounds. The Ravel CLI and worker enforce their own copies. A change to the request map cannot raise a bound. Evaluation bounds end selection, even inside try. A harness run can instead return its own timeout or output-limit status as data. The JSON notice names each bound separately and records its origin.

See set bounds.

Request field / notice nameDefault or fixed valueAdjustment and consequenceTaught in
selectionMs / selection30,000 mstimeoutMs, then --timeout-ms, within 1,000–600,000. Ravel measures it from the worker spawn through the startup, the import, the loader and the selection. Expiry is selection_timeout, status 124, unless cancellation or failed harness cleanup takes precedence. The teardown time is additional.Selection deadline
contextBytes / context262,144 bytescontextBytes, then --context-bytes, within 1–8,388,608. The caller file and the compact delivered JSON with measured must both fit. If one does not fit, the code is context_too_large, status 3.Selection-context budget
sourceBytes / sourceThe smaller of 65,536 bytes and contextBytesA positive whole-number max_bytes of a host read can replace the default, up to contextBytes. Too many bytes, or a requested limit that is too large, cause source_too_large, status 3. No script try can prevent this refusal.Host reads
sources / sourcesFixed 256It applies separately to the measured reads, with the caller document and reads in every phase, and to the attributed sources array of the document. If either has more, the code is too_many_sources, status 3. A caught exception for a read bound still causes the refusal.Measured and attributed sources
messageBytes / messageFixed 1,048,576 bytesIt limits a host request, selection result or failure report from the worker. A message that is too large gives message_too_large, status 3. A delivered document has the selection-context budget instead.Protocol bounds
diagnosticsBytes / diagnosticsFixed 262,144 bytesThe combined standard output and standard error of the worker, with print and debug output. Output above the limit gives output_limit, status 3. Ravel retains the first 262,144 bytes and discards the rest while it stops the worker.Output bounds
harnessRuns / harnessRuns0 runsOnly budgets.harnessRuns changes it, within 0–16. A launched run consumes one. Exhaustion gives harness_budget_exhausted, status 3.Harness runs

from in a reported bound is default, fixed, settings.json or the flag that set the bound. If the selection-context budget makes a source limit lower, that limit gets the origin of the budget. If a read gave its own limit, a source-bound refusal reports max_bytes as its from. The report uses ms, bytes, sources or runs for its numeric field. See bounds and their inputs.

Configuration script and request

Contents of a configuration script

What must a configuration script contain? Its entry is a Rhai module. It exports two constants and defines a select function. Here is a short configuration script. It sends each review request to a fake agent and refuses every other kind.

Fragment: config.rhai of the example notices in chapter 5, “Read notices separately from results”. This block omits the fake agent review-agent and the command that runs the script. Chapter 5 runs this script as a complete example.

export const SCHEMA_VERSION = 4;
export const VERSION = "notice-demo";

fn select(request, context) {
    if request.kind == "review" {
        return #{
            status: "selected",
            program: "review-agent",
            args: [request.prompt],
            provider: "local",
            model: "fake",
            effort: "low",
            reason: "Reviews go to the fake agent.",
        };
    }
    #{
        status: "refused",
        code: "unknown_kind",
        message: "This configuration script has no rule for the kind " + request.kind + ".",
        remedy: "Use the kind review.",
    }
}

#{ … } is a Rhai map, a set of named values. The last expression of a Rhai function is its return value. Thus, select returns the refusal map when the kind is not review.

The entry exports constants and defines public functions with the parameter counts below. Other constants and functions can serve as helpers. Top-level statements run once before the loader and selection. See define a configuration script.

DeclarationContract
export const SCHEMA_VERSION = 4;Required integer 4. Another integer, such as 3, gives unsupported_version. A missing or mistyped export gives config_invalid, status 3.
export const VERSION = "...";Required nonblank owner-maintained string. The notice reports it independently of Ravel’s package version.
fn select(request, context)Required public function with two parameters. Returns a selected or refused map. The second argument is the delivered selection context or ().
fn load_context(request)Optional public function with one parameter. Returns selection context or a refusal before select. A return of () gives context_invalid.

Functions read top-level constants through global::NAME. They do not capture variables around them. Ravel checks undefined variables at compilation and refuses absent map properties at execution. Ravel disables eval. print writes to the script’s captured standard output, and debug writes to its captured standard error with source information. Both streams count towards the diagnostics bound and reach the caller’s standard error.

SelectionRequest

What does the configuration script receive? The Ravel CLI builds the selection request from the caller’s command and the effective bounds. The script receives it as the first argument of select, and of load_context if the script has one. The script can use each field to choose a route or to build the arguments.

The selection request is a Rhai map. Every documented field is present. An absent optional input has the value (), Rhai’s empty value, not an empty string. The request carries data and limits, never the session-result context.

Fragment: a real request, from the checkout build on 2026-11-02. The configuration script printed ravel::json_encode(request), and the Ravel CLI wrote that line on standard error. This block omits the other lines and the command. It shows the working directory of the other fragments of this chapter.

config stdout: {"context":null,"cwd":"/private/tmp/ch6","kind":"review","limits":{"contextBytes":262144,"diagnosticsBytes":262144,"harnessRuns":0,"messageBytes":1048576,"selectionMs":30000,"sourceBytes":65536,"sources":256},"originalPrompt":"Review the changes to src/parse.rs and tests/parse.rs in the last commit.\nCheck that the code is correct and that the tests cover each change.\n\nWhen the review is complete, end this session with one command:\n- If you found no problem, run: ravel end-session approved\n- If you found a problem, write your findings to review-notes.md.\n  Then run: ravel end-session changes-needed\n","params":{"repo":"ravel"},"prompt":"Review the changes to src/parse.rs and tests/parse.rs in the last commit.\nCheck that the code is correct and that the tests cover each change.\n\nWhen the review is complete, end this session with one command:\n- If you found no problem, run: ravel end-session approved\n- If you found a problem, write your findings to review-notes.md.\n  Then run: ravel end-session changes-needed\n","schemaVersion":4,"skills":null,"taskFile":null,"taskId":"T-17"}

The caller gave --kind review, --param repo=ravel, --task-id T-17 and --prompt-file review-prompt.txt, the prompt file of chapter 1’s review. The prompt keeps each newline of the file, also the last one, which JSON shows as \n. JSON shows each () as null, so context, skills and taskFile were () in the script. See read the request.

FieldValue and limitsTaught in
schemaVersionAlways the integer 4, the version of the interface between Ravel and the configuration script. The selection context has its own schemaVersion, 1.Read the request
kindRequired nonempty caller token, unchanged from --kind. Ravel defines no catalogue.Caller inputs
originalPromptRequired UTF-8 string, possibly empty, with a maximum of 1,048,576 bytes and no NUL. The caller bytes and newlines stay unchanged. Read this field when the script examines the task.Prompt delivery
promptRequired UTF-8 string: the prompt to give to the program. With skill delivery disabled, it holds the same bytes as originalPrompt. With delivery enabled, it is the prepared prompt. The configuration script chooses how to give it to its program.Prompt delivery
cwdThe caller’s working directory as data. Relative input paths and selected program paths resolve from it. The worker starts in a private directory. The script cannot change that directory or read files directly.Caller directory
paramsString values by caller-supplied name, or #{} if there are none. Names are nonempty without =. Ravel refuses a duplicate name. All names and values together have a maximum of 65,536 bytes.Caller parameters
taskFileAbsolute UTF-8 path from --task-file, or (). Ravel does not canonicalise or read it, and the file does not have to exist.Caller data
taskIdNonempty opaque UTF-8 string of at most 1,024 bytes, or (). It names no Ravel record.Caller data
contextThe caller’s checked selection context, or (), before Ravel attaches measured. A loader sees this input, also when it returns different data for select.Caller and delivered documents
skills() with skill delivery disabled. Otherwise a map with exactly descriptorPath, sources, directories and mandatory, as in the descriptor. It holds no prompt offsets.Read the request
limitsRequired map with the seven effective fields in Bounds. A change to this map cannot change enforcement.Effective bounds

Selection and refusal

Selected

What does the configuration script return when it chooses a program? It returns a selection: a map with status: "selected". The selected map is the command that select returns. program and args are the command that Ravel runs. The other fields are labels.

A label describes the choice of the script, but it does not configure the harness. For example, a script that selects Claude Code puts the model option of Claude Code in args. The model label only tells the caller which model the script chose. Ravel writes the labels in the routing notice on the caller’s standard error. The labels do not reach the program.

All seven fields are required, and Ravel refuses unknown fields. Ravel validates the shape, then resolves the program. It does not check that the prompt is in the arguments or that the labels describe the actual program. The contents entry shows a real selection, and the table gives its values. See select a command.

FieldAccepted value and effectTaught in
statusLiteral "selected".Select or refuse
programNonblank string without NUL. It is an absolute path, a path relative to the caller that contains /, or a bare name that Ravel finds through the caller’s PATH. It also supplies argv[0].Program resolution
argsArray of strings after program. Ravel accepts an empty array and empty strings, but not NUL. Each string is one argument. Ravel does no word splitting, shell expansion or template interpretation.Construct arguments
providerNonblank owner label for the model origin. Ravel reports it as given. Ravel does not infer it or use it to contact a provider.Selection labels
modelNonblank owner label. It does not have to occur in args, and it does not configure the selected program by itself.Selection labels
effortNonblank owner label. Ravel defines no effort levels and does not change this label into a flag.Selection labels
reasonNonblank explanation by the owner. Ravel reports it verbatim in routing diagnostics.Selection labels

Refused

You may want the configuration script to decline a request, for example a kind that has no route. The script then returns a refusal: a map with status: "refused". The refused map declines the request from select or load_context. All four fields are required, and Ravel refuses unknown fields. Ravel launches no final managed process and tries no other route.

A valid refusal reports Ravel’s config_refused, status 3, with the owner’s code beside it as configCode. The stage is selection for select and context for load_context. See return a refusal and refuse from a loader.

FieldAccepted value and effectTaught in
statusLiteral "refused".Return a refusal
codeNonblank owner-defined string. It is separate from the namespace of Ravel’s diagnostic codes.Return a refusal
messageNonblank explanation of what prevents selection.Return a refusal
remedyNonblank action that the caller or owner can take.Return a refusal

What does the caller receive after a refusal? The configuration script of the contents entry refuses the kind deploy with the code unknown_kind. The Ravel CLI writes the refusal on the caller’s standard error and exits with status 3. Standard output stays empty. The diagnostic codes entry shows the real text, and the JSON failure document entry shows the JSON form.

Diagnostic codes

Did a session fail with a code such as config_refused? Find the code in the tables below. Each row gives the stage, the status and what to do next. The Ravel CLI writes each failure on the caller’s standard error. Without --json, the first line has the form ravel: refused (CODE, stage STAGE):, and each detail follows on its own line.

Fragment: standard error of a refused request, from the checkout build on 2026-10-29. The configuration script of the contents entry, saved as refuse.rhai in /tmp/ch6, refused the kind deploy. This block omits the command and its status, 3.

ravel: refused (config_refused, stage selection): the configuration script /private/tmp/ch6/refuse.rhai refused the selection: This configuration script has no rule for the kind deploy.
  input: --kind deploy
  source: /private/tmp/ch6/refuse.rhai
  config code: unknown_kind
  remedy: Use the kind review.

config_refused is Ravel’s code. unknown_kind is the code of the owner, from the refusal map. Ravel supplies the first line up to “refused the selection:”, and the input and source lines. The configuration script supplies the rest of the first line, the config code and the remedy.

With --json, the same failure is a JSON failure document. In a JSON failure document, error.code, error.stage and error.exit identify a Ravel failure. These tables give the usual classification of each code.

Cancellation has precedence over a failure that already occurred. Failed evaluation harness cleanup can replace a deadline with status 5. After evaluation, the original error decides the code and status. Ravel reports later cleanup errors beside it. Thus, a code in these tables can also occur as retained detail beside a different outcome.

Read the complete standard error stream. See interpret notices.

CodeStageStatusMeaning and remedyTaught in
malformed_inputcli2Invalid CLI input, numeric setting or grant name. Correct the named input and range. An invalid setting fails even with a valid flag.Inputs, settings
prompt_unreadablecli2Ravel cannot open or read the prompt file, or the file is a terminal. Supply a readable file that is not a terminal, or use --prompt.Prompt inputs
prompt_invalidcli2The prompt is invalid UTF-8, contains NUL or is larger than 1 MiB. Correct the encoding or the content, or make the prompt shorter.Prompt inputs
settings_invalidcli2The settings file, a key or a nested entry is invalid, or an object repeats a member name. Correct the named location to the six optional settings and their documented shapes.Settings and bounds
excluded_grantcli2A grant in the settings or in a flag names a reserved variable or prefix: a dynamic-loader control or one of Ravel’s own names. Remove the grant.Grants
confinement_withdrawncli2The command line holds --confine or --runtime-read, which Ravel 2.0.0 refuses. Remove both flags, and select the sandbox of the harness in the configuration script. Ravel 1.1 keeps --confine.What the managed process receives
exit_dir_unusablecli2The parent of the result channel is unusable. Name a writable directory that exists, or omit --exit-dir.Channel placement
cwd_unavailableauthority3Ravel cannot get the caller’s directory. Run from a readable directory that exists.Caller directory
home_unsetauthority3The default entry has no absolute HOME. Set an absolute HOME or use --config.Define a configuration script
config_missingauthority3The chosen entry does not exist. Create it or correct --config. Ravel does no search.Define a configuration script
config_unreadableauthority3The entry is not a readable regular UTF-8 file within 1 MiB, or its path or extension is unusable. Supply a valid .rhai entry.Define a configuration script
worker_missingworker5Ravel cannot locate or re-execute its own binary as the Rhai worker. Repair the installation or execute permissions.Validate with fake agents
worker_identity_mismatchworker5The re-executed worker reports a different protocol, package, build or Rhai identity. Reinstall Ravel and start a new invocation.Validate with fake agents
worker_failedworker5The preparation, execution or required teardown of the worker failed. Examine the retained operation details, and repair the installation or the named resource.Operational failures
protocol_errorworker5The private worker conversation is malformed or closed unexpectedly. Retain the diagnostics and reinstall or rebuild Ravel.Validate with fake agents
selection_timeoutevaluation124The whole deadline expired during worker startup, imports, loader, selection or host calls. Reduce the work or increase the permitted deadline. Failed harness cleanup can replace this outcome.Settings and bounds
selection_cancelledevaluation129/130/143HUP, INT or TERM ended the selection. Retain the teardown reports, and try again only if the caller still wants the work. Termination by the signal is the usual outcome at the process level.Selection cancellation
output_limitevaluation3The standard output and standard error of the worker together are larger than 262,144 bytes (256 KiB). Print less. The retained prefix is diagnostic evidence, not an accepted selection.Output bound
config_import_failedload3A module read, import cycle, module-count breach or top-level execution failed. Correct the file, import chain or top-level code.Import other Rhai files
config_invalidvalidation3A required export is absent or mistyped, or a function is private or has the wrong parameter count. Supply the exports and functions of the contents entry.Define a configuration script
unsupported_versionvalidation or context3The SCHEMA_VERSION of the configuration script is not 4, or the schemaVersion of the selection context is not 1. Supply the supported shape, not only its version number.Define a configuration script
message_too_largeselection3A host request, selection result or failure frame exceeds the 1 MiB worker-message bound. Reduce the data. The delivered selection context has its separate budget.Settings and bounds
context_unreadablecontext3Ravel cannot read the caller document as a regular file with a canonical UTF-8 path. Correct --context or omit it.Selection context
context_invalidcontext3The caller or loader document is invalid JSON, or it has invalid fields or data. Or the loader returned nothing, unserialisable data or a malformed refusal. Correct the named location to valid selection context or a valid refusal.Selection context
context_too_largecontext3The caller bytes, or the delivered JSON with the measurements, are larger than the budget. Deliver less, or increase contextBytes or --context-bytes within its range.Selection-context budget
context_loader_failedcontext3An ordinary error escaped load_context. Correct the source or handle the data error. No selection follows without the required selection context.Selection context
source_too_largecontext3A source exceeds its read limit, or max_bytes exceeds the context budget. Read less or use an allowed limit. No try can prevent refusal.Selection context
too_many_sourcescontext3Measured reads, with the caller document and failed UTF-8 or JSON conversion, exhausted 256 sources. Or attributed sources exceed 256. Read or attribute fewer sources.Selection context
config_refusedselection or context3select or the loader returned a valid refusal. Follow its configCode, message and remedy, which the owner supplied. No alternative route runs.Refusals, loader refusals
selection_abstainedselection3select returned (). Return a selected or refused map on every path.Select or refuse
selection_malformedselection3The result has the wrong shape, fields or types, or contains NUL, or Ravel cannot serialise it. Correct the named location to Selected or Refused.Selection shape
selection_threwselection3An ordinary error escaped select. Correct the reported source location or handle the data error. To decline intentionally, return a refusal map.Select or refuse
config_parse_failedload3The entry or an imported module does not compile. Correct the syntax or undefined variable at the source location.Validate with fake agents
config_format_retiredauthority3The selected entry uses a retired format. Supply a Rhai configuration script, as chapter 4 teaches. No compatibility path runs.Define a configuration script
config_reentry_refusedauthority3start-session inherited RAVEL_CONFIG_RUN from an evaluation harness run. Call the function of the route in the configuration script directly, not another session.Harness runs
host_request_invalidload, context or selection3A host call has an invalid argument type, field or value. Correct the function and field at the reported location. This failure is not catchable.Data helpers
script_limitevaluation3The configuration script exceeded an engine bound. Reduce its operations, recursion or data size. A try block cannot suppress this failure.Settings and bounds
memory_limitevaluation3The worker exceeded its 1 GiB heap bound. Reduce retained data. The worker exits, and no managed launch follows.Settings and bounds
harness_budget_exhaustedevaluation3The configuration script requested a harness run with no runs left. Reduce calls or raise budgets.harnessRuns in the settings, at most to 16. No caller flag raises it.Harness runs
harness_cleanup_failedevaluation5A harness run left a group, failed reap or failed required cleanup. Retain the reports and inspect the named resources. This failure replaces an evaluation deadline.Harness runs
program_not_foundresolution127The selected path does not exist, or no entry of the caller’s PATH has the bare name. Correct the path or PATH.Program resolution
program_unexecutableresolution126The selected file is not an executable regular file, or Ravel cannot search the caller’s PATH for a bare name. Correct the permissions, the file or PATH.Program resolution
CodeStageStatusMeaning and remedyTaught in
cancellation_unavailableexec5The Ravel CLI cannot prepare for cancellation. Examine the reported OS error and the execution environment.Operational failures
signal_state_unavailableexec5The capture of the signal state at entry, or a required transition of the signal state, failed. Retain all reports, and correct the execution environment. Do not treat any result as success.Know when Ravel cannot guarantee cleanup

session_channel_unavailableexec5Ravel cannot allocate or prepare the private result channel. Correct the temporary storage or --exit-dir, and examine the reports of a partial cleanup.Channel placement
supervision_failedexec127, 126 or 5Before the runner reports the start, ENOENT gives 127, and other failures give 126. After the start, the status is 5. Check the executable and the interpreter, the execution environment and the retained reports of the runner and the terminal.Launch and supervision
session_result_missingexec1The managed process ended and sent no result, even if its own status was 0. Make the managed process call end-session when its work succeeds.The sent result and its absence
session_result_invalidexec5Ravel cannot accept the sent entry in the result channel or the result bytes, for example because of an invalid encoding, type or size. Use end-session inside the live session, and send a result of 1 MiB or less.Result validity
session_cleanup_failedexec5The required cleanup of the result channel or of a private directory failed. Examine the named path and the ownership report before you remove any residue. Ravel returns no result to the caller.Know when Ravel cannot guarantee cleanup
process_group_survivedexec5Ravel did not confirm the absence of the group when it decided the final outcome. Examine the reported group and the cleanup failures before you try again. The sent result alone is not sufficient.Cleanup and success
session_cancelledexec129/130/143HUP, INT or TERM has priority when Ravel decides the final outcome, even after Ravel received the result. Ravel returns no result to the caller. Retain the original and cleanup errors. Try again only if the caller still wants the work.Cancellation
result_write_failedexec5The final write or flush of the result failed after Ravel decided the outcome. Correct the output destination, and discard any prefix that Ravel already wrote.Result output
CodeStageStatusMeaning and remedyTaught in
skill_source_missingskills3A local source directory or a configured search directory does not exist. Or a mandatory file names a source that the default skill directory does not hold. Restore or install the source, or change the settings deliberately.skills resolve
skill_source_changedskills3A local source changed while Ravel copied it. Keep the source quiet, then try again.skills resolve

An end-session failure is a plain ravel: end-session: message with status 1. It is not an entry in the JSON code table. A missing, empty or stale RAVEL_EXIT_FILE, an oversized argument or a failed send does not mean an empty result. See send and accept results.

Selection context and host capabilities

Context and DeliveredContext

You may want facts about the task to change the choice of the configuration script. For example, a large change can go to a higher effort. Selection context carries such facts to select, as its second argument. It is data for the decision, not authority to execute a command. Do not confuse it with the session-result context, the path in RAVEL_EXIT_FILE.

Fragment: a real caller document and the delivered selection context that select received, from the checkout build on 2026-10-29. The caller gave --context context.json, and the configuration script printed ravel::json_encode(context). This block omits the command and the other lines on standard error.

$ cat context.json
{"schemaVersion":1,"summary":"Review the change","facts":{"effort":"high"}}
config stdout: {"facts":{"effort":"high"},"measured":[{"bytes":76,"name":"/private/tmp/ch6/context.json","sha256":"695ce021a52f9af91d5b1b71e5571bbe1096b0c9543f108c8fc7864453512d88","via":"--context"}],"schemaVersion":1,"summary":"Review the change"}

The script had no load_context, so select received the caller’s document. Ravel added measured, with the size and the SHA-256 digest of the 76 bytes that it read. The script can now read context.facts.effort and choose the effort high.

Selection context is a JSON document. --context supplies it, or load_context returns it. An absent optional field means unknown, which is different from a known empty object, array or string. Ravel refuses unknown top-level fields, for example program, args, argv, command, select, load_context, env, environment, exec, shell and script. These names inside free-form JSON facts stay data. See supply selection context.

FieldAccepted valueTaught in
schemaVersionRequired number 1. The selection request has its own schemaVersion, 4.Selection-context shape
summaryOptional string, which can be empty.Selection-context shape
acceptanceCriteriaOptional array of strings. The array and the strings can be empty.Selection-context shape
factsOptional object that maps names to JSON values. A fact is data. Ravel never runs it as a command.Selection-context shape
assessmentsOptional object that maps names to attributed { by, value } objects.Attributed judgements
sourcesOptional array of 256 or fewer attributed source records. These records are separate from measured reads. A record does not cause the read of a file.Source attribution

The delivered selection-context map adds measured to the accepted document. Neither the caller nor the loader supplies that field. Without a loader, selection receives the caller document with the measurements, or () if there is no document.

A loader replaces the document that select receives, but request.context still gives the caller’s original input. Ravel does not merge the two automatically. Ravel refuses a loader that returns nothing. See caller and delivered documents.

measured holds the successful reads in order. The caller’s --context document comes first when present, then successful load_context reads, also repeated reads of one file. Top-level and selection reads appear only in the notice’s reads list.

The separate limit of this list is 256 records. The compact encoding of the whole delivered document, with this list, must fit contextBytes. Source attribution and measurement do not prove who the author was or that a claim is true. See measurement and its limits.

Nested data and measured records

This entry gives the shapes inside a selection-context document: assessments, source records and the records of measured reads. Use it when Ravel refuses a document as context_invalid and names a field.

JSON data allows null, booleans, finite numbers, strings, arrays and objects of JSON values. Ravel cannot deliver cycles, executable values or other values that are not JSON. An object whose only key is $ravel is ordinary JSON data. See JSON data in selection context.

Type and fieldAccepted value or meaningTaught in
Assessment.byRequired nonblank string that names who made the judgement.Assessments
Assessment.valueRequired JSON value. Null is valid. Ravel refuses unknown assessment fields.Assessments
SourceRecord.nameRequired nonblank name, such as a path, URL or evidence label. It does not cause a read.Attributed sources
SourceRecord.sha256Optional digest of exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits. At least this field or version is required.Attributed sources
SourceRecord.versionOptional nonblank version string. At least this field or sha256 is required. Ravel accepts both.Attributed sources
SourceRecord.bytesOptional nonnegative whole-number byte count. Ravel refuses unknown source-record fields.Attributed sources
MeasuredSource.nameRequired canonical file path of the source that Ravel measured.Measured sources
MeasuredSource.viaRequired "--context", "read_text" or "read_json". It identifies the read.Measured sources
MeasuredSource.bytesRequired count of the bytes that Ravel actually read, before it parses any JSON.Measured sources
MeasuredSource.sha256Required lowercase SHA-256 digest of those bytes.Measured sources
ReadSource.nameRequired canonical file path that the host returns beside the content of a read.Host reads
ReadSource.bytesRequired actual byte count that the host returns.Host reads
ReadSource.sha256Required lowercase SHA-256 of the bytes that the host read. The configuration script can put the three-field object in the attributed sources.Host reads

Host capabilities

A configuration script cannot read a file, read the environment or run a program by itself. When it needs one of these effects, it calls a host capability, a ravel:: function that Ravel gives to the script. For example, ravel::read_json reads a JSON file, and ravel::run_harness runs a harness.

How does a host call work? The Rhai worker sends the request to the Ravel CLI over a private socket and waits. The Ravel CLI checks the request, does the work and sends the answer back. Then the script continues with the returned value. The two reads, env, harnesses, remaining and run_harness work in this way. The data helpers, json_parse, json_encode, json_check, uuid_v4, path_resolve and path_parent, compute inside the worker and send no request.

The ravel namespace supplies the functions below at the module’s top level, in load_context and in select. Calls are synchronous, so only one host call runs at a time. See host capabilities.

FunctionArguments, return value and failure
ravel::read_text(path)Read a nonempty string path to a regular UTF-8 file. A relative path uses request.cwd. Return #{ text, source }, with the source record described above.
ravel::read_text(path, max_bytes)The same read with an explicit positive integer byte limit, at most contextBytes. The default is the smaller of 65,536 and contextBytes.
ravel::read_json(path)The same read and measurement, then a strict JSON parse. Return #{ value, source }. JSON depth is at most 100 levels.
ravel::read_json(path, max_bytes)The same JSON read with the explicit limit described for read_text.
ravel::json_parse(text)Parse a string as strict JSON. Return its Rhai value. Invalid JSON or depth beyond 100 levels throws json_invalid.
ravel::json_encode(value)Return canonical JSON text, with sorted object keys and no whitespace. A value without a JSON form throws json_unencodable.
ravel::json_check(value, schema)Check the JSON Schema subset below and return the value unchanged. Throw schema_invalid, json_unencodable or json_mismatch on failure.
ravel::uuid_v4()Return a random version-4 UUID string in lowercase hexadecimal. If the operating system supplies no random bytes, throw random_unavailable.
ravel::path_resolve(base, path)Both arguments are strings. Join a relative path to an absolute base, then normalise it. An absolute path replaces the base. A relative base throws path_not_absolute.
ravel::path_parent(path)Return the parent of an absolute string path. Remove final slashes and the last segment. The parent of / is /. A relative path throws path_not_absolute.
ravel::env(name)Read a string name from the caller’s permitted environment. Return its string value or () when unset. Throw env_not_granted or env_not_utf8 on failure.
ravel::harnesses()Return the settings entries in name order, with the discovery fields below. Resolve programs without execution or network access.
ravel::remaining()Return the milliseconds left, run budget and source count in the fields below.
ravel::run_harness(request)Run the named settings entry with the request below. Return the run result after cleanup. A budget breach or cleanup failure ends evaluation.

The read functions throw catchable maps for absent, non-regular, unreadable, non-UTF-8 or invalid-JSON sources. The data-error table gives each code. A map has code, message and a name when applicable. A wrong argument type or malformed host request ends evaluation as host_request_invalid. Rhai resolves function arity before the host function runs. A wrong argument count therefore raises an ordinary function-call error instead.

A source consumes one count when its bytes arrive, even if UTF-8 or JSON conversion fails. A read with no file consumes none. Repeated reads each consume one. Only successful reads enter the measurement records. Entry and module loads have their own module bound and do not consume this source count.

A read above its byte bound gives source_too_large. A read after the source count reaches 256 gives too_many_sources. Neither failure is catchable. A requested max_bytes above contextBytes also ends evaluation. The source-count check occurs before the read, also a request for an absent file.

The path helpers read no file and resolve no symlink. path_resolve removes empty and . segments and applies .. without a step above /. path_parent keeps the spelling that remains: /a//b gives /a/, and /a/./b gives /a/.. See data helpers for their use.

JSON values and schema checks

A configuration script often handles JSON: a selection-context document, a file that it reads or the answer of a model. You may want to check that an answer has the shape that your script needs before you use it. ravel::json_check does this. This entry gives the conversion between Rhai values and JSON, and the schema keywords that the check accepts.

Values that cross between the configuration script and Ravel use this conversion. Arrays and maps nest at most 100 levels. A conversion failure identifies the value’s location, such as result.args[2]. See data conversion.

Rhai valueJSON value or outcome
()null.
BooleanBoolean.
Signed 64-bit integerInteger. A JSON integer outside this range becomes a float on input.
Finite floatNumber. Ravel refuses NaN and infinities.
StringString.
ArrayArray of converted values.
MapObject of converted values.
Character, BLOB, function pointer, timestamp, range or custom typeRefused. These values have no JSON form at this boundary.

json_parse rejects comments, final commas, single-quoted strings, NaN and text after the value. json_encode sorts object keys by code point and writes no whitespace. It escapes quotes, backslashes and control characters with JSON escapes. Other characters remain UTF-8. Finite floats use the shortest form that reads back exactly and retains their float type. These helpers have different rules from Rhai’s built-in parse_json and to_json.

json_check accepts only the keywords below. The root schema has level 1. Each nested property schema or items schema adds one level, up to 32. An unknown keyword or unsupported schema shape throws schema_invalid. A mismatch throws json_mismatch. Its location can contain property names, but its message omits property values.

KeywordAccepted value and effect
typeOne of object, array, string, number, integer, boolean, null, or an array of those names. An integral float such as 3.0 matches integer.
propertiesObject from property names to schemas. Applies to objects.
requiredArray of property names that an object must contain.
additionalPropertiesBoolean that permits or refuses object properties outside properties.
itemsSchema for each array item.
enumArray of scalar JSON values: strings, numbers, booleans or nulls.
title, descriptionStrings that the check ignores.

Object keywords impose no requirement on an array, and items imposes none on an object. Use type when the value must have a particular kind. A schema checks data shape and permitted values. The configuration script still decides their meaning.

Discovery and available budgets

You may want the configuration script to choose only a harness that is installed on this computer. ravel::harnesses() reports the harness entries of the settings without execution. The Ravel CLI finds each program as it finds a selected program, and it tells the script whether it found an executable file. It reports availability, not a recommendation or live model list.

Fragment: the value that ravel::harnesses() returned with the settings of the settings entry, from the checkout build on 2026-10-29. The configuration script printed it with ravel::json_encode. This block omits the command and the other lines.

config stdout: [{"available":true,"capabilities":["classify"],"name":"classifier","program":"fake-classifier","reason":null,"resolved":"/tmp/ch6/bin/fake-classifier"}]

Each map contains these fields. See discover harnesses.

FieldValue
nameSettings entry name. Entries appear in name order.
programThe owner’s program string.
availableBoolean: whether program resolution found an executable regular file.
resolvedResolved path when available, otherwise ().
reason() when available. Otherwise not_found or not_executable.
capabilitiesThe owner’s string labels, unchanged. An omitted setting gives an empty array.

You may want the configuration script to decide whether it has time and budget for one more harness run. ravel::remaining() returns the following map for this evaluation. It changes no budget. Each nested session in the final managed process has its own budgets. See evaluation bounds.

FieldValue
msMilliseconds before the whole evaluation deadline.
harnessRunsThe run budget of the settings minus the runs that started.
sources256 minus the caller’s context document and host reads whose bytes arrived. Failed UTF-8 or JSON conversion still consumes a source.

Harness-run request

You may want an LLM to choose the best harness, model, effort and prompt additions for a kind and a prompt. You can do this with a harness run in your configuration script. A harness run is one run of a program from your harnesses settings, during evaluation. Its answer goes back to the script, not to the caller. The script then makes its own choice.

How does the answer get back to the script? Follow one harness run:

OrderWho actsWhat happens
1The configuration scriptCalls ravel::run_harness with the name of a settings entry, the arguments and the text for standard input
2The Rhai workerSends the request to the Ravel CLI over its private socket and waits
3The Ravel CLIFinds the entry’s program, checks the run budget and runs the program in a new POSIX session with its own result channel
4The harness runReads its standard input, writes its answer on standard output and exits
5The Ravel CLIStops the processes that remain in the group of the run, completes cleanup and sends a map back to the worker
6The configuration scriptReads status, exitCode and stdout from the map, then continues

The harness run returns its answer to the configuration script before Ravel launches the final managed process.

Figure: The evaluation harness run and the managed process are separate launches.

Open the evaluation harness sequence at full size.

A harness run differs from the managed process. The managed process is the final program, which Ravel launches after evaluation. Its result goes to the caller. A harness run happens during evaluation, and its output goes only to the configuration script.

ravel::run_harness(request) executes a program that the harnesses settings name. It evaluates no configuration script and selects no final command. The request is a map with these fields and no others. See run a harness.

FieldContract
harnessRequired string name of a settings entry. An unknown name returns unavailable.
argsRequired array of strings after the entry’s program. Empty arrays and strings are valid. NUL is not. Each string is one argument.
stdinOptional string for standard input. Without it, input is at end of file.
timeoutMsOptional positive integer in milliseconds. Ravel lowers it to the time left for evaluation. Without it, all the time left applies.

The configuration script supplies every argument and decides whether the prompt belongs in an argument or standard input. Ravel builds no harness-specific argument and parses no harness output. An LLM call uses this same operation. The harness uses its own sign-in. Ravel holds no model credential or token budget.

Each run uses the caller’s working directory, with a new POSIX session and no terminal. It uses the managed-process environment rules. Ravel does not restrict its filesystem access. It receives RAVEL_CONFIG_RUN and a fresh RAVEL_EXIT_FILE for its own result channel. A result sent there reaches only the configuration script, not the outer session’s caller.

Each launched run consumes one from budgets.harnessRuns. An unavailable call consumes none, even with no budget left. After successful program resolution, a call with no budget left ends evaluation before launch. A malformed request also ends evaluation, as host_request_invalid. The script cannot catch these evaluation failures.

Harness-run result

The call returns after the run’s required cleanup. A natural exit does not need an end-session value. The configuration script interprets the status, output and optional result.

Fragment: the map that ravel::run_harness returned for a fake classifier, from the checkout build on 2026-10-29. The classifier wrote review and exited. The configuration script printed the map with ravel::json_encode. This block omits the command and the other lines.

config stdout: {"elapsedMs":389,"error":null,"exitCode":0,"result":null,"signal":null,"status":"exited","stderr":"","stdout":"review\n"}

The run exited with status 0 after 389 milliseconds. It did not use end-session, so result was (), which JSON shows as null. Its answer was on standard output. The value of elapsedMs changes in each run.

Every returned map has the following fields. See interpret harness results.

FieldValue and meaning
statusOne of the five statuses in the next table.
exitCodeInteger process exit code when available, otherwise (). A nonzero code is data for the script.
signalSignal name such as SIGTERM, or ().
stdoutCaptured text, at most 1 MiB of source bytes. Invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD.
stderrCaptured text, with the same independent 1 MiB bound and UTF-8 replacement rule.
resultText sent with end-session, or () when no result exists. An empty string is a sent result.
elapsedMsInteger elapsed milliseconds. An unavailable call reports zero.
error() except for unavailable, which supplies #{ code, message }. The codes appear below.
StatusMeaning
exitedThe program exited. Inspect exitCode, output and any result.
signalledA signal ended the program. Inspect signal.
timed_outThis run’s deadline expired. Ravel stopped the run and completed cleanup. Evaluation can continue if its whole deadline remains.
output_limitOne output stream exceeded 1 MiB. Ravel stopped the run and kept the bounded output. Evaluation can continue.
unavailableNo program started. Output strings are empty, result and termination fields are (), and error explains why.

A result sent with end-session starts the same 2-second grace and 5-second TERM grace as a final managed process. A per-run timeout requests TERM immediately, with KILL after the kill grace if necessary. Cleanup covers descendants that stay in the run’s process group. A group that survives, a failed reap or failed required temporary-directory cleanup ends evaluation as harness_cleanup_failed, status 5. The script cannot catch that failure.

Host data errors

Your configuration script can handle some failures of a host call itself. For example, a missing file can mean “use the default route”.

These codes reach the script as data. They have no independent CLI exit status. A thrown helper error can reach catch. If it escapes, Ravel reports config_import_failed, context_loader_failed or selection_threw, as the evaluation phase requires. Unavailable-run errors instead appear in run.error. See catchable errors and harness outcomes.

CodeOrigin and remedy
source_missingA measured read found no file. Supply the file or handle its absence.
source_not_regularThe source is not a regular file. Supply a regular file, not a directory or FIFO.
source_unreadableThe read, canonical path or encoded reply is unusable. Check permissions, path encoding and source content.
source_not_utf8Source bytes do not decode as UTF-8. Correct the file encoding. The read still consumes one source.
source_invalid_jsonread_json found invalid JSON or depth beyond 100 levels. Correct the data. The read still consumes one source.
json_invalidjson_parse found invalid strict JSON or excessive depth. Correct the text.
json_unencodableA value given to json_encode or json_check cannot cross the JSON boundary. Correct the named value or its depth.
schema_invalidjson_check received an unsupported schema. Use the listed keywords and depth bound.
json_mismatchA value fails json_check. Inspect its named location and handle the unusable value.
path_not_absoluteA path helper needs an absolute base or argument. Supply the caller’s absolute directory or another absolute path.
env_not_grantedravel::env cannot read that name. Grant it explicitly if permitted, or remove the read.
env_not_utf8The permitted caller variable contains non-UTF-8 bytes. Correct the caller value.
random_unavailableuuid_v4 received no random bytes from the operating system. Handle the failure or correct the OS resource problem.
unknown_harnessrun_harness names no settings entry. Correct the entry name or add the intended entry.
harness_not_foundThe configured program does not resolve. Correct its path or the caller’s PATH.
harness_not_executableThe configured program is not executable. Correct its file type or execute permissions.
harness_launch_failedThe runner could not start the configured program. Inspect the message, interpreter and execution environment.

Module resolution

Import resolution

You may want to keep a route table or a helper function in one file and use it from several configuration scripts. A Rhai import statement does this.

import "PATH" as NAME; loads a file module. A relative import resolves from the directory of the file that imports it. An absolute import uses its own path. An entry that is a symlink uses the directory of its real file. See import other Rhai files.

Import propertyRule
ExtensionRavel appends .rhai when the path has no extension.
FileReadable regular UTF-8 file, at most 1 MiB, with a canonical UTF-8 name.
Repeated importEach distinct canonical file loads once per evaluation. Repetition does not consume another module.
CountAt most 64 distinct imported modules, besides the entry.
CycleRavel refuses an import cycle as config_import_failed.
MeasurementThe notice lists the entry first, then modules in first-import order, with canonical name, bytes and SHA-256.
Embedded modulesNone. Every import names a file. The ravel host namespace needs no import.

A missing, unreadable, oversized or cyclic module gives config_import_failed. A syntax error gives config_parse_failed with its source location. The importer cannot catch a failed host import and continue selection.

Engine and conversion bounds

What stops a configuration script that loops for ever or makes too much data? The Rhai engine counts the operations, the call depth and the size of the data of a script. Ravel sets a limit for each of these counts. When the script reaches a limit, evaluation ends and Ravel launches no final managed process.

Ravel uses the same bounds in both development and release builds. An engine limit ends evaluation as script_limit, status 3. The worker heap bound gives memory_limit, status 3. A try block cannot catch either, also inside an array or map callback. See evaluation bounds.

BoundFixed value and consequence
Operations100,000,000 across evaluation.
Function call depth64 levels.
Expression depth64 at top level, 32 inside a function.
String size16,777,216 bytes (16 MiB).
Array size1,048,576 elements, with nested elements.
Map size1,048,576 properties, with nested properties.
Variables16,384 at one time.
Script functions4,096.
Worker heap1,073,741,824 live allocated bytes (1 GiB).
Worker evaluation stack64 MiB. A worker abort is an operational failure, not a successful selection.
Imported modules64 files besides the entry. A 65th distinct module gives config_import_failed.
Entry or module source1,048,576 bytes per file. Entry failure gives config_unreadable, and module failure gives config_import_failed.
Encoded host-read reply19,922,944 bytes (19 MiB), with JSON escapes and record fields. Excess throws catchable source_unreadable and consumes one source.
JSON conversion depth100 levels of arrays and maps. The boundary reports a data-conversion error at the value’s location.
JSON Schema depth32 levels, with the root at level 1. Excess gives catchable schema_invalid.
Host calls at one timeOne, because each host call is synchronous.
Harness output1,048,576 bytes per stream. Excess returns run status output_limit after cleanup.
One harness deadlineThe smaller of its positive timeoutMs and the whole time left. Expiry can return timed_out.

Rhai checks container size when a call returns a value. An in-place change can exceed that container limit before such a check. The worker heap bound still applies. At the whole evaluation deadline, Ravel ends evaluation and stops the worker, even if a native call does not yield.

At cancellation or the whole deadline, Ravel abandons a read and does not wait for its reply. It stops an active harness run before it stops and reaps the worker. Ravel waits at most 8 seconds for harness cleanup: the 5-second kill grace, 2 seconds for a failed reap and 1 second for group confirmation. If the runner has not completed cleanup by that limit, Ravel reports harness_cleanup_failed. Worker teardown follows and has its own 1-second grace before KILL.

Kernel stalls can still delay process exit. These bounds are not a real-time guarantee.

Example configuration scripts

You may want to begin from a configuration script that already runs, instead of an empty file. Ravel installs three example configuration scripts for this purpose.

The examples are editable Rhai files under share/ravel/examples/. Their checkout source is crates/ravel/examples/. That directory also holds harness-table.rhai, an example for Ravel’s own tests, which Ravel does not install. Copy the files that your chosen example imports, then replace the placeholder commands and labels. Ravel installs no personal configuration script and has no init command. See copy a configuration script and its files.

FileBehaviour and dependencies
static.rhaiExports SCHEMA_VERSION 4, VERSION ravel/examples/static 3 and ROUTES. agent(effort) constructs a route, and select_route(routes, request) applies it. select uses the exported table.
sample.rhaiImports adjacent static.rhai. Version owner-choice-1. Reads .ravel-choice in request.cwd and applies the choices below.
llm.rhaiImports adjacent static.rhai. Version llm-triage-1. Calls the claude harness entry with a 20,000 ms timeout and a schema restricted to table keys.

static.rhai selects my-agent-wrapper, provider your-provider and model your-model. Its arguments are --model, the model, --effort, the effort, and the unchanged prompt.
